0 (constant per trial) | `behavior.get_trial_types` (isreward) | |\n| `general/subject/subject_id` | subjects / subject_idx | m3..m19 | - | 11 subjects |\n| `general/optophysiology/ImagingPlane/location` | brain_regions | all cells CA1 | - | single region |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Neural signal = deconvolved events recomputed from F/Fneu** (not the stored `Deconvolved` series): the paper and\n   all reference analyses (GLM, Fig. 3 decoder) use OASIS events derived from the per-trial maximin dF/F. The stored\n   NWB `Deconvolved` series is suite2p deconvolution of raw F (no neuropil correction, no per-trial baseline), so it\n   would not match the reference processing.\n2. **Time bin = one imaging frame (64.4836 ms)**, no re-binning: the reference pipeline processes all neural and\n   behavioural data at the imaging frame rate (~15.5 Hz) and the NWB behavioural series are already interpolated\n   onto those frame times, so this preserves exact temporal alignment with no resampling.\n3. **Trial window `[trial_start-1, teleport-1)`** exactly as in `preprocessing.dff` and\n   `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`; alignment event = trial start (entry to the linear track), off_start = -0.0645 s\n   (the window begins one frame before the trial-start flag), off_end = None (variable trial length).\n4. **Cell curation**: iscell==1 (suite2p manual curation) and exclusion of putative interneurons by dF/F-speed\n   Pearson r > 0.5, both as in the reference. Planes pooled for m17/m18 (paper pools planes).\n5. **No speed threshold**: the reference drops samples with speed < 2 cm/s for its GLM/decoder, but speed is a\n   required decoder output here (with an explicit <2 cm/s class) and the decoder needs contiguous trial time series,\n   so all within-trial samples are kept. This is a required deviation, documented here.\n6. **Dropped trials**: (a) the 81 lick-sensor-error trials (>30% of frames with cumulative lick count > 2), because\n   lick is a required output and the reference treats these lick values as invalid (NaN); (b) the first lap of each\n   session (152 trials), because previous-trial outcome is a required input and is undefined for it (the preceding\n   warm-up laps are not in the imaging record). Remaining trials: ~11,980 of 12,216 (98.1%).\n7. **Per-trial variables are stored as constant time series** (shape (d, T)) so that every trial has a uniform\n   (d_input, T) / (d_output, T) layout, and the instruction to make outputs time-varying where possible is honoured.\n8. **Reward zone per trial from the scene name with the switch at lap 30** (reference `behavior.get_reward_zones`),\n   verified against the data-derived reward-zone onset position in every session.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [x] Trial counts: 12,216 complete laps, 80.37 +/- 6.16 per session (paper: 80.5 +/- 7.4), 11 mice, 152 sessions.\n- [x] Lick-error rule reproduces the paper 81 trials exactly.\n- [x] Cells/session 155-2341 (paper 155-2172) and 138,678 total before interneuron exclusion.\n- [ ] Scene-derived reward zone matches the data-derived reward-zone onset position in every session (per-session assert).\n- [ ] Fraction of cells excluded as putative interneurons ~0.4-1% (paper 0.42 +/- 0.85%).\n- [ ] Reward outcome fraction ~84.7% rewarded / 15.3% omission.\n- [ ] Environment: 9 mice start in ENV1, 2 (m17, m18) in ENV2; environment flips at lap 30 only on cross-env days.\n- [ ] Output distributions: position bins roughly uniform (~20% each); distance-to-zone bin 3 (inside zone) ~11%\n  (50 cm of the 450-cm track); speed mostly in 10-40 cm/s bins; licks on a small fraction of frames.\n- [ ] Spot-check raw-data sanity checks with np.allclose on neural, input and output values (Step 10).\n- [ ] dF/F values in a plausible range (about -1 to 10) and events non-negative.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`/app/convert_data.py` implements the conversion. Structure:\n- `load_session(fn)`: reads one NWB file with h5py: behavioural series, frame timestamps, sparse Reward\n  timestamps, trial_start/teleport frame indices, scene name from `identifier`, and the iscell-filtered\n  F / Fneu matrices of every imaging plane (concatenated over planes).\n- `compute_dff_and_events(...)`: port of `reward_relative.preprocessing.dff` with the reference settings\n  (neuropil subtract 0.7 with per-trial neuropil mean added back, per-trial maximin baseline:\n  gaussian sigma=15 frames, minimum then maximum filter of 300 frames (~20 s), dF/F = (F-base)/|base|,\n  gaussian sigma=2 frames), then per-trial OASIS deconvolution (`suite2p.extraction.dcnv.oasis`,\n  batch 2000, tau 0.7, fs = imaging_rate/n_planes).\n- `nansmooth`: port of `reward_relative.utilities.nansmooth`.\n- interneuron exclusion: vectorised Pearson correlation between each cell dF/F and speed over the\n  within-trial samples, threshold 0.5 (port of `spatial.is_putative_interneuron`, method=speed).\n- `scene_reward_zones(scene, n_trials)`: port of `behavior.get_reward_zones` (dictionary A/B/C zones,\n  switch after 30 laps, handles both `Env1_LocationA_to_C` and `Env1_C_to_Env2_B` scene styles).\n- trial-type computation mirrors `behavior.get_trial_types` (rewarded = reward delivered AND reward-zone\n  flag active) and the Methods lick-sensor-error rule (>30% of frames with cumulative lick count > 2).\n- `reward_zone_distance` / `digitize_rz_distance` implement the required output discretisation.\n- `--show-processing` writes three figures per session: (1) raw F, neuropil F, dF/F, events, position with\n  the reward zone shaded, speed and licks/rewards over four consecutive laps with trial start/teleport\n  lines, so temporal alignment of every stream is visible; (2) per-trial comparisons of each continuous\n  behavioural variable with its discretised output plus session-wide value-vs-bin scatter plots (proving\n  the discretisation) and the converted neural raster next to position; (3) per-lap reward zone, reward\n  outcome, previous outcome and environment.\n- Sessions are processed in parallel (`multiprocessing`, default 8 workers, `maxtasksperchild=1` to keep\n  memory bounded); each worker returns only the converted per-trial arrays.\n\nCode inefficiencies identified: naive per-cell correlation loop for interneuron detection; float64\nintermediates for F/Fneu.\nCode speedups added: vectorised correlation (matrix-vector product), OASIS on float32 arrays, reading only\niscell columns from HDF5, parallel sessions.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nCommand: `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing`\n(sample = the two smallest sessions from two different mice: m11 day 4, m19 day 2).\n\n### Sample Statistics\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Sessions | 2 |\n| Neurons (total) | 572 (575 iscell, 3 = 0.52% excluded as putative interneurons) |\n| Neurons / session | 167, 405 |\n| Subjects | 2 (m11, m19) |\n| Sessions / subject | 1 |\n| Trials (total) | 158 of 160 laps (2 first laps dropped, 0 lick-error laps) |\n| Trials / session | 79, 79 |\n| Timepoints / trial | mean 206, min 147, max 476 |\n| time_from_trial_start range | [-0.064, 30.6] s |\n| environment range | [0, 0] (both sessions ENV1) |\n| trial_number range | [1, 79] |\n| previous_trial_outcome range | [0, 1] |\n| reward_zone_distance distribution | [0.259, 0.106, 0.088, 0.253, 0.020, 0.070, 0.205] |\n| position distribution | [0.232, 0.174, 0.151, 0.301, 0.142] |\n| speed distribution | [0.110, 0.076, 0.160, 0.222, 0.432] |\n| lick distribution | [0.816, 0.184] |\n| reward_zone_location distribution | [0.440, 0.000, 0.560] (zone A in m11 day 4, zone C in m19 day 2) |\n| reward_outcome distribution | [0.212, 0.788] |\n| dF/F range | [-0.21, 4.97] |\n| median reward-zone onset error | 0.63 cm (data-derived zone entry vs scene-derived zone start) |\n\n### Processing Plots Review\n`processing_m11_ses-04_*.png` and `processing_m19_ses-02_*.png`:\n- raw F and neuropil traces are continuous; Both streams start at\nt = 0, so the extra imaging frame is at the end; `load_session` now truncates all streams to the common length\n(and prints a NOTE). A second issue was found in the same run: in 8 sessions (again only m17/m18) dF/F reached\n|dF/F| ~ 3000 for a handful of cells. Diagnosis: for those cells the neuropil trace is much larger than the ROI\ntrace (e.g. Fneu 4960 vs F 1487), so after the reference neuropil subtraction the corrected trace crosses zero\nand the per-trial maximin baseline collapses to ~0; dF/F = (F - base)/|base| then explodes. Because the decoder\nPCA would be dominated by such numerically degenerate cells, cells whose minimum within-trial baseline is below\n5% of their median raw fluorescence are now excluded (25 cells = 0.018% of all curated cells). After the fix the\ndF/F range over kept cells is [-16.0, 37.2] and the maximum deconvolved event is 7.1.\n\n### Consistency Check\n| Statistic | Reference Paper | Reference Code | Reference Data (NWB) | Converted Data | Match? |\n|-----------|-----------------|----------------|----------------|----------------|--------|\n| Subjects | 11 switch mice | anim lists include mice not in DANDI | 11 | 11 | yes |\n| Sessions | 14 days/mouse, m11 from day 3 | sessions_dict: 14 days/mouse | 152 | 152 | yes |\n| Sessions per subject | 14 (m11: 12) | 14 | 14 (m11: 12) | 14 (m11: 12) | yes |\n| Total neurons (curated) | 155-2172 per session | iscell | 138,678 iscell | 138,244 after interneuron (409) and unstable-baseline (25) exclusion | yes |\n| Mean neurons/session | - | - | 912.4 | 909.5 (min 154, max 2319) | yes |\n| Trials (total) | 12,376 imaged | - | 12,216 complete laps | 11,983 (12,216 - 81 lick-error - 152 first laps) | expected |\n| Trials/session (mean) | 80.5 +/- 7.4 | - | 80.37 +/- 6.16 | 78.8 (min 39, max 99) | yes |\n| Lick-error trials | 81 (0.65%) | rule with thr 0.35/0.5 | 81 at the paper thr 0.30 | 81 dropped | exact |\n| Rewarded trials | omissions ~15% | isreward rule | 84.7% | 84.3% of frames rewarded; The only messages in `/app/conversion_full_out.txt` are the informational NOTE\nlines for the 10 m17/m18 sessions where the ophys stream is one frame longer than the behavioural stream\n(handled by truncation, see Step 9).\n\n### Check 2: Independent sanity checks (`/app/cache/sanity_checks.py`)\nThese checks load the raw NWB files directly and re-derive quantities with code written independently of\n`convert_data.py`, then compare with `np.allclose`. Three sessions were chosen to cover the different session\ntypes: `m11 ses-03` (1 plane, within-environment reward-zone switch B->A), `m3 ses-08` (cross-environment\nswitch `Env1_C_to_Env2_B`) and `m18 ses-13` (2-plane mouse, one of the sessions with the frame-count offset).\nAll 39 checks passed (`0 checks failed`):\n- **neural**: an independent re-implementation of the reference dF/F (neuropil subtraction, per-trial maximin\n  baseline, sigma=2 smoothing) + OASIS deconvolution, plus the two curation rules, reproduces the number of\n  kept cells exactly (154/154, 778/778, 1427/1427) and the stored event matrices for the first, middle and last\n  trial of each session (`np.allclose`, atol 1e-5). A single-value spot check\n  (`neural[trial 5, neuron 3, timepoint 10]`) also matches, e.g. 0.022363 for m11 ses-03.\n- **input**: `time_from_trial_start` equals `timestamps[start-1:stop-1] - timestamps[start]` for the first,\n  middle and last trial; `environment` equals the NWB environment value of the lap; `trial_number` equals the\n  NWB lap index; `previous_trial_outcome` equals the independently recomputed `isreward` of the preceding lap\n  for every trial of the session.\n- **output**: every one of the six outputs was recomputed for every kept trial from raw NWB position/speed/lick\n  and from the scene-derived reward zone, and matched exactly (reward-zone distance bins, position bins, speed\n  bins, lick binary, reward-zone location, reward outcome).\n- **structure**: `subject_idx` matches the per-session subject, `brain_region_idx` lengths match the neuron\n  counts, and sampled neural arrays contain no NaN/Inf.\n\n### Check 3: Reference code comparison\n| Step | Reference | This conversion | Same? |\n|------|-----------|-----------------|-------|\n| (a) data loading | `TwoPUtils.sess` + `vr_align_to_2P` build `sess.vr_data` (per imaging frame) and `sess.timeseries` from suite2p F/Fneu | NWB behavioural series are exactly that aligned product; F/Fneu read from `processing/ophys` | yes (the NWB files are the published form of `sess`) |\n| (b) neuron filtering | `iscell` from suite2p manual curation; `spatial.is_putative_interneuron(ts_key='dff', method='speed', r_thresh=0.5)` | identical rules (iscell + dF/F-speed r > 0.5); plus an extra numerical-stability rule (25 cells, 0.018%) for cells whose maximin baseline collapses to ~0 | yes + documented addition |\n| (c) temporal alignment | trial window `[trial_start-1, teleport-1)` in `preprocessing.dff` and `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data`; all streams share the imaging frame clock | identical window, identical clock, and inputs/outputs are taken from exactly the same frames as the neural data | yes |\n| (d) binning | no re-binning; everything at the ~15.5 Hz frame rate (paper: all time series sampled at ~15.5 Hz) | one frame per time bin, 64.4836 ms | yes |\n| (e) input construction | `glmUtils.get_timeseries_data` builds trials/pos/speed/licks/rewarded per frame | same source variables; decoder inputs are the four required by the task (time from trial start, environment (= `morph`), lap index (= `trials`), previous-lap outcome (= `isreward` shifted by one)) | yes, restricted to the task spec |\n| (f) output construction | `rel_pos` = distance from `rzone[i,0]` (`behavior.get_reward_zones`); `pos`; `speed`; binary `licks` (`licks[licks>1]=1`); `isreward` (`behavior.get_trial_types`); `rz label` | same variables, with the discretisation required by the task; distance is to the nearest point of the 50 cm zone (task asks for distance to *any* location in the zone) rather than to the zone start | yes, with the task-mandated discretisation |\n\nDifferences and their reasons:\n1. **No 2 cm/s speed threshold.** The reference drops sub-threshold samples for place-cell, GLM and decoder\n   analyses. Here speed is an output with an explicit `< 2 cm/s` class and the decoder needs contiguous trial\n   time series, so all within-trial samples are kept.\n2. **Distance measured to the nearest point of the reward zone** (0 throughout the zone) instead of the\n   reference RR position relative to the zone start, because the task specifies bins around 0 for the zone.\n3. **Linear (not circular) distance**: the paper decoder used circular RR coordinates; the task specifies\n   linear signed distance bins in cm.\n4. **Extra exclusions**: 81 lick-error laps (the reference NaNs their lick values, which is not possible for a\n   required output) and the first lap of each session (no previous-lap outcome).\n5. **Extra neuron rule** for numerically unstable dF/F baselines (0.018% of cells), needed because the decoder\n   applies PCA to raw event amplitudes.\n\n### Check 4: Key statistics comparison\nSee the table in Step 9. Highlights: 11 subjects, 152 sessions (14/mouse, m11 12), 12,216 complete laps in the\ndata vs 12,376 imaged trials quoted by the paper (the NWB release contains 160 fewer laps), 80.37 +/- 6.16 laps\nper session vs 80.5 +/- 7.4 in the paper, 155-2341 curated cells per session vs 155-2172 quoted, exactly 81\nlick-sensor-error laps as in the paper, 84.7% rewarded laps (paper: omissions on a ~15% subset), interneuron\nexclusion 0.29% (paper 0.42 +/- 0.85%), frame period 64.4836 ms (paper 0.0645 s), reward zones at 80/200/320 cm\nverified against the measured zone-entry position (median error 0.78 cm).\n\n### Check 5: Edge cases\n- Frame-count mismatch between ophys and behaviour in 10 sessions: detected and handled by truncation; the\n  affected sessions now convert and pass the independent sanity checks (m18 ses-13 above).\n- `trial_start` at frame 0 would make the `[start-1, ...)` window invalid: verified never to occur.\n- Trials never contain pre-synchronisation samples (`trial number` = -1, position = -500), non-scanning samples,\n  or teleport-period samples (position = -50); asserted on all 152 sessions.\n- Sessions with fewer than 2 usable trials would break decoder validation: the conversion drops such sessions\n  (none occurred; the smallest session keeps 39 laps).\n- Non-finite event values would fail format verification: each trial is checked with `np.all(np.isfinite(...))`\n  before being stored (none were dropped).\n- Laps beyond lap 30 in switch sessions get the post-switch zone; sessions with fewer than 30 laps (m4 ses-04,\n  41 laps) are handled because the zone list is built with `min(CHANGE_TRIAL, n_trials)`.\n- The last lap of a session is complete (paired teleport) in every session, so no partial lap is included.\n\n---\n\n## Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nCommand: `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --plot-samples`\n(ran to completion on the GPU (NVIDIA L4); log in `/app/train_decoder_full_out.txt`, figures\n`sample_trials.png` and `predictions.png`).\n\n### Training Progress\n- Loss decreasing: Yes. 2.377 (epoch 10) -> 1.429 (50) -> 1.185 (100) -> 1.043 (150) -> 0.935 (200);\n  held-out test loss 0.799 (below the final training loss, so no overfitting).\n\n### Decoder Results (Full: 152 sessions, 11,983 trials, 138,244 neurons)\n| Output | Chance | Training Balanced Acc | Validation Balanced Acc | Validation / chance | Notes |\n|--------|--------|-------------|--------|-------|-------|\n| reward_zone_distance (7 classes) | 0.143 | 0.611 | 0.534 | 3.7x | strongest relative decoding, as expected from the paper reward-relative code |\n| position (5 classes) | 0.200 | 0.698 | 0.645 | 3.2x | classic CA1 place coding |\n| speed (5 classes) | 0.200 | 0.630 | 0.583 | 2.9x | |\n| lick (2 classes) | 0.500 | 0.780 | 0.758 | 1.5x | |\n| reward_zone_location (3 classes) | 0.333 | 0.891 | 0.854 | 2.6x | per-trial label; identifiable from the session/zone-specific code |\n| reward_outcome (2 classes) | 0.500 | 0.798 | 0.590 | 1.18x | weakest; see Step 12 Check 1 for the analysis |\n\nComparison with the paper: the paper only reports a circular decode score (cosine similarity, 1 = perfect,\n0 = chance) for reward-relative position, not classification accuracies, so no direct numeric comparison is\npossible. Qualitatively the ordering matches the paper findings: reward-relative distance and track position\nare robustly decodable from CA1 deconvolved activity, well above chance.\n\n---\n\n## Step 12: Critical Review 2\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis\n| Variable | Chance | Validation acc | Ratio | Assessment |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| reward_zone_distance | 0.143 | 0.534 | 3.74x | good |\n| position | 0.200 | 0.645 | 3.23x | good |\n| speed | 0.200 | 0.583 | 2.92x | good |\n| lick | 0.500 | 0.758 | 1.52x | good (binary variable, ceiling is lower) |\n| reward_zone_location | 0.333 | 0.854 | 2.56x | good |\n| reward_outcome | 0.500 | 0.590 | 1.18x | below 1.5x -> investigated below |\n\nNo output is below chance.\n\n**Investigation of `reward_outcome` (`/app/cache/diag_outcome.py`, `/app/cache/diag_outcome_decode.py`).**\n1. Definition check on the raw data: across all 12,216 laps there is **no** lap with a delivered reward but no\n   reward-zone flag, and 52 laps with a zone flag but no reward (the reference `get_omission_trials` calls these\n   lapsed trials, distinct from true omissions). So my `isreward` equals the reference\n   `behavior.get_trial_types` rule and equals simply whether a reward was delivered.\n2. `autoreward` is identically zero in every NWB file, so no extra information is available there.\n3. Causal structure: on rewarded laps the reward is delivered on average 40.7% of the way through the lap\n   (median 40.4%). Before that moment the animal has not yet experienced the outcome, so a frame-wise decoder\n   cannot know it; the label is nevertheless defined for the whole lap because it is a per-trial variable.\n4. Direct test: a 19-session decoder run (`diag_outcome_decode.py`) split validation frames by reward-zone\n   distance bin. Balanced accuracy for `reward_outcome` was **0.531 before the reward zone** (distance bins 0-2)\n   and **0.637 in/after the reward zone** (bins 3-6), versus 0.598 overall. The information is therefore present\n   in CA1 activity only once the animal reaches the zone, exactly as expected for the physiology (the paper\n   Fig. 6 contrasts rewarded vs omission trials *after* reward-zone entry). The modest overall number is a\n   property of the task and of a frame-wise per-trial label, not a conversion error.\n5. Class balance is 84.3% rewarded / 15.7% omitted, which is the true behavioural rate (paper: omissions on a\n   ~15% subset of laps), so this is not a degenerate label either.\n\n### Check 2: Accuracy comparison to paper\nEvery decoding result in the paper (Fig. 3, Extended Data Fig. 3) is expressed as a circular-linear decode\nscore (mean cosine of the angular error) or as a z-score relative to a 100-shuffle null, for the decoding of\nreward-relative position from selected cell classes (RR, track, non-RR remapping). The paper reports no\nclassification accuracy for any variable, and it never decodes position, speed, licking, reward-zone identity\nor reward outcome. A numeric table comparing accuracies is therefore not possible. The qualitative expectation\nfrom the paper - that reward-relative position is decodable well above chance from deconvolved CA1 events -\nis reproduced (3.7x chance, the best of all outputs in relative terms).\n\n### Check 3: Train vs validation gap\n| Output | Train | Validation | Train/Val |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| reward_zone_distance | 0.611 | 0.534 | 1.14 |\n| position | 0.698 | 0.645 | 1.08 |\n| speed | 0.630 | 0.583 | 1.08 |\n| lick | 0.780 | 0.758 | 1.03 |\n| reward_zone_location | 0.891 | 0.854 | 1.04 |\n| reward_outcome | 0.798 | 0.590 | 1.35 |\nAll ratios are below 1.5, so there is no evidence of severe overfitting or data leakage; the largest gap\n(reward_outcome, 1.35) is expected for a per-trial label with only ~79 labels per session (the network can\npartly memorise training laps, but cannot do so for held-out laps).\n\n### Additional debugging checks performed\n- Output values verified against the raw NWB data for three whole sessions, trial by trial (Step 10, Check 2).\n- Temporal alignment verified visually (`processing_*_timeseries.png`, `processing_*_outputs.png`): the neural\n  raster, position, speed and licks of a single trial are plotted on the same time axis and the trial\n  boundaries coincide; find nans\n    99→    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n   100→    a_nanless = np.copy(a)\n   101→    # make nans 0\n   102→    a_nanless[nan_inds] = 0\n   103→\n   104→    # inversely weight nanned indices\n   105→    one = np.ones(a.shape)\n   106→    one[nan_inds] = .001\n   107→    a_nanless = ndimage.gaussian_filter(a_nanless, sig) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n   108→    one = ndimage.gaussian_filter(one, sig) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n   109→    return a_nanless / one\n   110→\n   111→\n   112→def nansmooth_1d(a, sig, axis=-1, return_nans=False, **kwargs):\n   113→    \"\"\"\n   114→    apply Gaussian smoothing to matrix A containing nans with kernel sig\n   115→    without propagating nans\n   116→    :param a:\n   117→    :param sig:\n   118→    :return:\n   119→\n   120→    ** copied from src/reward_relative/utilities.py::nansmooth **\n   121→    (renamed: the TwoPUtils N-D version above already occupies the name `nansmooth`)\n   122→    \"\"\"\n   123→\n   124→    # find nans\n   125→    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n   126→    a_nanless = np.copy(a)\n   127→    # make nans 0\n   128→    a_nanless[nan_inds] = 0\n   129→\n   130→    # inversely weight nanned indices\n   131→    one = np.ones(a.shape)\n   132→    one[nan_inds] = 0.001\n   133→    a_nanless = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(a_nanless, sig, axis=axis, **kwargs) # That is their code, unchanged.\n   170→\n   171→    ** copied from src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py::dff **\n   172→    Keeps only the branches this dataset can reach (single-channel)\n   173→    Dropped: bleedthrough_ts, neu_bleedthrough_ts, regress_ts, scrub_ts, neuropil_method='regress',\n   174→    baseline_method='maxsmooth', subtract_baseline=False. \n   175→    \"\"\"\n   176→\n   177→    print('baseline method =', baseline_method)\n   178→\n   179→    # Check frame rate if deconvolving\n   180→    if deconvolve:\n   181→        if (frame_rate/n_planes) > 16:\n   182→            warnings.warn(\n   183→                f\"Using frame rate per plane: {(frame_rate/n_planes)} -- is this correct?\")\n   184→\n   185→    f_ = np.zeros(f.shape) * np.nan\n   186→\n   187→    if f_neu is not None:\n   188→        f_neu_ = np.zeros(f_neu.shape) * np.nan\n   189→\n   190→    if keep_teleports:\n   191→        # if keeping the teleports, keep all the fluorescence from the start of imaging\n   192→        # EXCEPT the teleport sample itself, as this position values might be interpolated between\n   193→        # the end of the track and -50 (where the teleport jitter starts)\n   194→        print(\"keeping teleports\")\n   195→        start_inds = np.append(\n   196→            trial_starts[0],\n   197→            np.array(teleports[:-1] + 2)\n   198→        ).tolist()\n   199→        # ^ add 2 so with start-1 we will start 1 sample after the teleport\n   200→        stop_inds = teleports.to_list()\n   201→        # we will subtract 1 off this index below\n   202→    else:\n   203→        # if not keeping the teleports, keep only the fluorescence on each trial\n   204→        start_inds = trial_starts.tolist()\n   205→        stop_inds = teleports.tolist()\n   206→\n   207→    for i, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(start_inds, stop_inds)):\n   208→        f_[:, start - 1:stop -\n   238→                 1] = nansmooth(f_[:, start-1:stop-1], [0, 15])\n   239→\n   240→            # minimum filter, taking min val over 20 sec\n   241→            flow[:, start-1:stop-1] = ndimage.minimum_filter1d(\n   242→                flow[:, start-1:stop-1], int(300), axis=-1) # was sp.ndimage.filters.minimum_filter1d\n   243→            flow[:, start-1:stop-1] = ndimage.maximum_filter1d(\n   244→                flow[:, start-1:stop-1], int(300), axis=-1)  # max filter with same window (dilation), was sp.ndimage.filters.maximum_filter1d\n   245→\n   246→    # to get deltaF/F: subtract baseline from initial signal, divide by abs(baseline)\n   247→    # baseline can some times end up as negative due to regression\n   248→    dff = np.zeros(f_.shape) * np.nan\n   249→    if subtract_baseline:\n   250→        dff[:, nanmask] = (f_[:, nanmask] - flow[:, nanmask]\n   251→                           ) / np.abs(flow[:, nanmask])\n   252→\n   253→    print('dff:', dff.shape, 'min ', np.nanmin(dff), 'max ', np.nanmax(dff))\n   254→\n   255→    # Smooth the deltaF/F transients by 2 time bins\n   256→    for i, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(start_inds, stop_inds)):\n   257→        dff[:, start - 1:stop -\n   258→            1] = nansmooth_1d(dff[:, start - 1:stop - 1], 2, axis=1)\n   259→        # After smoothing: Get \"spikes\" with calcium kernel deconvolution from suite2p\n   260→        if deconvolve:\n   261→            spks[:, start-1:stop-1] = dcnv.oasis(\n   262→                np.ascontiguousarray(dff[:, start-1:stop-1], dtype=np.float32),\n   263→                2000, tau, (frame_rate/n_planes)) # this is float64 in the original code\n   264→\n   265→    if deconvolve:\n   266→        return dff, spks\n   267→    else:\n   268→        return dff\n   269→\n   270→\n   271→def is_putative_interneuron(dff, speed, r_thresh=0.5):\n   272→    \"\"\"\n   273→    Find putative interneurons based on correlation of dF/F with speed\n   274→    (under the hypothesis that fast-spiking interneurons in the pyramidal\n   275→    layer are often highly correlated with running speed).\n   276→\n   277→    :param dff: (ncells, T) dF/F timeseries, NaN outside the analysed windows\n   278→    :param speed: (T,) running speed in cm/s\n   279→    :param r_thresh: threshold for speed correlation r; dayData sets int_thresh = 0.5, which is\n   280→        the value the Methods quote (\"a Pearson correlation of >0.5\"). Note that the threshold\n   281→        defaulted to 0.3 in the source function; the analyses always pass 0.5.\n   282→    :return: is_int (array) with a Boolean for each ROI, where putative interneuron is True.\n   283→\n   284→    ** copied from src/reward_relative/spatial.py::is_putative_interneuron **\n   285→    Change: takes the dF/F and speed arrays as arguments instead of reading them off a TwoPUtils \n   286→    `sess` object, which this conversion does not build.\n   287→    \"\"\"\n   288→    speed_corr = np.zeros((dff.shape[0],))\n   289→    nanmask = ~np.isnan(dff[0, :])\n   290→\n   291→    for c in range(dff.shape[0]):\n   292→        speed_corr[c] = np.corrcoef(\n   293→            dff[c, nanmask], speed[nanmask])[0, 1]\n   294→\n   295→    is_int = speed_corr > r_thresh\n   296→\n   297→    return is_int\n   298→\n   299→# end copy-paste\n   300→# =================================================================================================\n   301→\n   302→\n   303→def get_subjects(datadir=DATADIR):\n   304→    # find all directories in datadir that start with 'sub-'\n   305→    subjects = [d.split('-')[1] for d in os.listdir(datadir) if d.startswith('sub-')]\n   306→    return subjects\n   307→\n   308→def get_sessions(subject, datadir=DATADIR):\n   309→    subject_dir = os.path.join(datadir, f\"sub-{subject}\")\n   310→    # parse sessions from files named sub-<subject>-<session>_behavior+ophys.nwb\n   311→    nwb_files = [f for f in os.listdir(subject_dir) if f.endswith('.nwb')]\n   312→    sessions = [int(f.split('_')[1].split('-')[1]) for f in nwb_files]\n   313→    return sessions\n   314→\n   315→def get_nwb_file(subject, session, datadir=DATADIR):\n   316→    nwb_file_path = os.path.join(datadir, f\"sub-{subject}\", f\"sub-{subject}_ses-{session:02d}_behavior+ophys.nwb\")\n   317→    assert os.path.exists(nwb_file_path), f\"File {nwb_file_path} does not exist\"\n   318→    return nwb_file_path\n   319→\n   320→def interp(new_idx,old_idx,old_data,**kwargs):\n   321→    f = interp1d(old_idx, old_data, axis=0, **kwargs)\n   322→    return f(new_idx)\n   323→\n   324→\n   325→def get_trial_starts_ends(trial_start,teleport):\n   326→    trial_start_idx = np.nonzero(trial_start)[0]\n   327→    is_teleport_start = (teleport[1:] > 0) & (teleport[:-1] <= 0)\n   328→    trial_end_idx = np.nonzero(is_teleport_start)[0] + 1 \n   329→    assert len(trial_start_idx) == len(trial_end_idx), f\"Number of trial starts ({len(trial_start_idx)}) does not match number of trial ends ({len(trial_end_idx)})\"\n   330→    return trial_start_idx, trial_end_idx\n   331→        \n   332→\n   333→def survey_session_data(subject, session, datadir=DATADIR):\n   334→    nwb_file_path = get_nwb_file(subject, session, datadir)\n   335→    info = {}\n   336→    info['reward_zone_positions'] = []\n   337→    with pynwb.NWBHDF5IO(nwb_file_path, 'r') as io:\n   338→        nwbfile = io.read()\n   339→        # parse neural activity data\n   340→        deconvolved_data = nwbfile.processing[\"ophys\"][\"Deconvolved\"]\n   341→        spike_data = []\n   342→        nplanes = len(deconvolved_data.roi_response_series)\n   343→        for plane_data in deconvolved_data.roi_response_series.values():\n   344→            roi_data = plane_data.rois[:]\n   345→            iscell = np.array(roi_data['iscell'].to_list())[:,0] == 1\n   346→            spike_data_curr = plane_data.data[:]\n   347→            assert spike_data_curr.shape[1] == iscell.shape[0], f\"Number of neurons in data ({spike_data_curr.shape[1]}) does not match number of iscell entries ({iscell.shape[0]})\"\n   348→            spike_data.append(spike_data_curr[:,iscell])\n   349→        spike_data = np.concatenate(spike_data, axis=1)\n   350→        info['T'], info['nneurons'] = spike_data.shape\n   351→        max_spike_rate = np.max(spike_data)\n   352→        info['max_spike_rate_range'] = (max_spike_rate.min(), max_spike_rate.max())\n   353→        info['neural_time_bin_size'] = nplanes/plane_data.rate*1000 # ms\n   354→\n   355→        behavior_data = nwbfile.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series       \n   356→        istrial = behavior_data['trial number'].data[:] >= 0\n   357→        # reward_amounts are all the same\n   358→        reward_amounts = behavior_data['Reward'].data[:]\n   359→        info['unique_reward_amounts'] = np.unique(reward_amounts)\n   360→        info['nrewards'] = len(reward_amounts)\n   361→        # autoreward is all 0s, so not useful for modeling, but check that it is all zeros\n   362→        autoreward = behavior_data['autoreward'].data[:]\n   363→        info['unique_autoreward'] = np.unique(autoreward[istrial])\n   364→        # vr environment\n   365→        vr_environment = behavior_data['environment'].data[:] # one value per time point, does this change by session?\n   366→        info['unique_vr_environment'] = np.unique(vr_environment[istrial])\n   367→        licks = behavior_data['lick'].data[:]\n   368→        info['unique_licks'] = np.unique(licks[istrial])\n   369→        position = behavior_data['position'].data[:]\n   370→        info['position_range'] = (position[istrial].min(), position[istrial].max())\n   371→        reward_zone = behavior_data['reward_zone'].data[:]\n   372→        info['unique_reward_zone'] = np.unique(reward_zone[istrial])\n   373→        scanning = behavior_data['scanning'].data[:]\n   374→        info['unique_scanning'] = np.unique(scanning[istrial])\n   375→        speed = behavior_data['speed'].data[:]\n   376→        info['speed_range'] = (speed[istrial].min(), speed[istrial].max())\n   377→        teleport = behavior_data['teleport'].data[:]\n   378→        info['unique_teleport'] = np.unique(teleport[istrial])\n   379→        \n   380→        # trial number and trial start do not agree with each other\n   381→        # use trial_start and teleport to find starts and ends of trials\n   382→        trial_start_idx, trial_end_idx = get_trial_starts_ends(behavior_data['trial_start'].data[:], teleport)\n   383→        trial_length = trial_end_idx - trial_start_idx\n   384→        info['trial_length_range'] = (trial_length.min(), trial_length.max())\n   385→\n   386→        ntrials = len(trial_start_idx)\n   387→        info['ntrials'] = ntrials\n   388→        info['reward_zone_positions'] = np.full((ntrials, 2), np.nan)\n   389→        for trial in range(ntrials):\n   390→            idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n   391→            pos_trial = position[idx]\n   392→            reward_zone_trial = reward_zone[idx] > 0\n   393→            if not np.any(reward_zone_trial):\n   394→                continue\n   395→            info['reward_zone_positions'][trial] = [np.min(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial]), np.max(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial])]\n   396→                    \n   397→    return info\n   398→    \n   399→def get_bin_centers(bin_edges,data):\n   400→    # replace infinity with the max/min of the data for plotting purposes\n   401→    bin_edges_plot = np.array(bin_edges.copy())\n   402→    if np.isinf(bin_edges_plot[0]):\n   403→        bin_edges_plot[0] = data.min()\n   404→        if bin_edges_plot[0] > bin_edges_plot[1]:\n   405→            bin_edges_plot[0] = 2*bin_edges_plot[1] - bin_edges_plot[2] # extrapolate to get a lower value for the first bin edge\n   406→    if np.isinf(bin_edges_plot[-1]):\n   407→        bin_edges_plot[-1] = data.max()\n   408→        if bin_edges_plot[-1] < bin_edges_plot[-2]:\n   409→            bin_edges_plot[-1] = 2*bin_edges_plot[-2] - bin_edges_plot[-3] # extrapolate to get a higher value for the last bin edge\n   410→    bin_centers = (bin_edges_plot[:-1] + bin_edges_plot[1:]) / 2\n   411→    return bin_centers\n   412→    \n   413→def compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position_curr, reward_zone_curr):\n   414→    ntimepoints = len(position_curr)\n   415→    reward_zone_range = reward_zone_dict[reward_zone_curr]\n   416→    distance_to_reward_zone = np.zeros(ntimepoints)\n   417→    idx_before_zone = position_curr < reward_zone_range[0]\n   418→    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_before_zone] = position_curr[idx_before_zone] - reward_zone_range[0] # negative if before the zone\n   419→    idx_after_zone = position_curr > reward_zone_range[1]\n   420→    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_after_zone] = position_curr[idx_after_zone] - reward_zone_range[1] # positive if after the zone\n   421→    return distance_to_reward_zone\n   422→    \n   423→def convert_session_data(subject, session, info, trial_offset, time_bin_size=None, datadir=DATADIR, \n   424→                         show_processing=False, max_neurons_plot=30, min_ntimepoints=50,\n   425→                         figfile=None):\n   426→\n   427→    nwb_file_path = get_nwb_file(subject, session, datadir)\n   428→    io = pynwb.NWBHDF5IO(nwb_file_path, 'r')\n   429→    nwbfile = io.read()\n   430→\n   431→    # parse neural activity data: the raw suite2p traces the paper computes its dF/F and\n   432→    # deconvolved events from. The deconvolution needs the trial boundaries, so it runs further\n   433→    # below, once those have been parsed out of the behavior data.\n   434→    fluorescence_data = nwbfile.processing[\"ophys\"][\"Fluorescence\"]\n   435→    neuropil_data = nwbfile.processing[\"ophys\"][\"Neuropil\"]\n   436→    fluorescence = []\n   437→    neuropil = []\n   438→    nplanes = len(fluorescence_data.roi_response_series)\n   439→    for plane_name, plane_data in fluorescence_data.roi_response_series.items():\n   440→        roi_data = plane_data.rois[:]\n   441→        iscell = np.array(roi_data['iscell'].to_list())[:,0] == 1\n   442→        fluorescence_curr = plane_data.data[:]\n   443→        neuropil_curr = neuropil_data.roi_response_series[plane_name].data[:]\n   444→        assert fluorescence_curr.shape[1] == iscell.shape[0], f\"Number of neurons in data ({fluorescence_curr.shape[1]}) does not match number of iscell entries ({iscell.shape[0]})\"\n   445→        assert neuropil_curr.shape == fluorescence_curr.shape, f\"Neuropil shape ({neuropil_curr.shape}) does not match fluorescence shape ({fluorescence_curr.shape})\"\n   446→        # Subset to the curated cells now rather than after dff. Every step of dff is per-cell --\n   447→        # its only cross-cell operation is a Gaussian with sigma 0 on the cell axis -- so this is\n   448→        # numerically identical to subsetting afterwards, on a third of the memory.\n   449→        fluorescence.append(fluorescence_curr[:,iscell])\n   450→        neuropil.append(neuropil_curr[:,iscell])\n   451→    T = fluorescence[0].shape[0]\n   452→    # The rate stored on the series is the scanner rate: with two planes interleaved it is twice\n   453→    # the rate at which any one plane is sampled, which is why dff takes n_planes as well.\n   454→    frame_rate = plane_data.rate\n   455→    neural_time_bin_size = nplanes/frame_rate*1000 # ms\n   456→\n   457→    behavior_data = nwbfile.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series\n   458→    \n   459→    timestamps = behavior_data['trial number'].timestamps[:] \n   460→    if T != len(timestamps):\n   461→        print(f\"Warning: number of neural time bins ({T}) does not match number of behavior timestamps ({len(timestamps)}), cropping to the minimum of the two\")\n   462→        T = min(T, len(timestamps))\n   463→        fluorescence = [f[:T] for f in fluorescence]\n   464→        neuropil = [f[:T] for f in neuropil]\n   465→        timestamps = timestamps[:T]\n   466→    \n   467→    behavior_time_bin_size = np.median(np.diff(timestamps))*1000 # ms\n   468→    \n   469→    # trial number for each time point\n   470→    trial_start_idx, trial_end_idx = get_trial_starts_ends(behavior_data['trial_start'].data[:], behavior_data['teleport'].data[:])\n   471→    ntrials = len(trial_start_idx)\n   472→    \n   473→    # reward_amounts are all the same\n   474→    reward_amounts = behavior_data['Reward'].data[:][:T]\n   475→    assert np.allclose(reward_amounts,reward_amounts[0]), f\"Reward amounts are not all the same, unique values: {np.unique(reward_amounts)}\"\n   476→    reward_times = behavior_data['Reward'].timestamps[:] # in seconds        \n   477→    # find the closest timestamp to reward times, not careful for mismatches right now\n   478→    reward_time_indices = np.searchsorted(timestamps, reward_times)\n   479→    err = timestamps[reward_time_indices] - reward_times\n   480→    assert np.all(np.abs(err) <= time_bin_size/2), f\"Reward times do not align with neural time bins, max error: {np.max(np.abs(err))} s\"\n   481→    isreward = np.zeros(T)\n   482→    isreward[reward_time_indices] = 1\n   483→\n   484→    # autoreward is all 0s, so not useful for modeling, but check that it is all zeros\n   485→\n   486→    # vr environment\n   487→    vr_environment = behavior_data['environment'].data[:][:T] # one value per time point, does this change by session?\n   488→    assert np.allclose(behavior_data['environment'].timestamps[:][:T], timestamps), \"Timestamps for environment do not match trial number timestamps\"\n   489→\n   490→    # lick\n   491→    licks = behavior_data['lick'].data[:][:T]\n   492→    assert np.allclose(behavior_data['lick'].timestamps[:][:T], timestamps), \"Timestamps for lick do not match trial number timestamps\"\n   493→\n   494→    # current position in the VR environment\n   495→    position = behavior_data['position'].data[:][:T]\n   496→    assert np.allclose(behavior_data['position'].timestamps[:][:T], timestamps), \"Timestamps for position do not match trial number timestamps\"\n   497→\n   498→    # teleport -- not sure what to do with this, maybe should end the trial early??\n   499→    #isteleport = behavior_data['teleport'].data[:][:T] == 1\n   500→    #assert np.allclose(behavior_data['teleport'].timestamps[:][:T], timestamps), \"Timestamps for teleport do not match trial number timestamps\"\n   501→\n   502→    # speed\n   508→    speed = behavior_data['speed'].data[:][:T] # cm / s\n   509→    assert np.allclose(behavior_data['speed'].timestamps[:][:T], timestamps), \"Timestamps for speed do not match trial number timestamps\"\n   510→\n   511→    io.close()\n   512→\n   513→    assert len(timestamps) == T, f\"Number of timestamps ({len(timestamps)}) does not match number of neural time bins ({T})\"\n   514→    if time_bin_size is not None:\n   515→        assert np.isclose(neural_time_bin_size, time_bin_size, rtol=1e-2), f\"Neural time bin size ({neural_time_bin_size} ms) does not match specified time bin size ({time_bin_size} ms)\"\n   516→        assert np.isclose(behavior_time_bin_size, time_bin_size, rtol=1e-2), f\"Behavior time bin size ({behavior_time_bin_size} ms) does not match specified time bin size ({time_bin_size} ms)\"\n   517→    \n   518→    # neural activity: the paper's deconvolved events, computed per plane because the\n   519→    # deconvolution kernel depends on the per-plane sampling rate, frame_rate/nplanes.\n   520→    #\n   521→    # The released subject id m<N> is the paper's internal animal GCAMP<N>: methods.txt describes\n   522→    # exactly the two groups this table holds -- \"except for mice m11-m14 on task days 1, 7, 8 and\n   523→    # Session\n   525→    # number is the experiment day: m11 starts at ses-03, and the paper says imaging for m11\n   526→    # started on day 3.\n   527→    keep_teleports = session in teleport_sessions.get(f'GCAMP{subject[1:]}', {}).get('exp_days', [])\n   528→    spike_data = []\n   529→    nint = 0\n   530→    for fluorescence_curr, neuropil_curr in zip(fluorescence, neuropil):\n   531→        # The trial indices are passed in shifted by +1: dff slices its windows as\n   532→        # [start-1, stop-1), one sample earlier than the [trial_start, teleport) laps emitted here\n   533→        # -- which is the window their own behavior.py:57 takes from the same two index arrays, so\n   534→        # the two files already disagree by a sample upstream. Unshifted, the last sample of every\n   535→        # lap comes out NaN (80 per session here) and one pre-lap sample gets a value instead;\n   536→        # shifted, dff's windows land exactly on the emitted laps. The one cost is that on\n   537→        # keep_teleports sessions the baseline window opens two samples after the teleport onset\n   538→        # rather than their one.\n   539→        dff_curr, events_curr = dff(fluorescence_curr.T.astype(np.float64),\n   540→                                    pd.Series(trial_start_idx + 1), pd.Series(trial_end_idx + 1),\n   541→                                    f_neu=neuropil_curr.T.astype(np.float64),\n   542→                                    neuropil_method='subtract', baseline_method='maximin',\n   543→                                    subtract_baseline=True, neu_coef=0.7, tau=0.7,\n   544→                                    frame_rate=frame_rate, n_planes=nplanes, deconvolve=True,\n   545→                                    keep_teleports=keep_teleports)\n   546→        # Drop the putative interneurons the paper excludes from further analysis.\n   547→        isint = is_putative_interneuron(dff_curr, speed)\n   548→        nint += int(np.sum(isint))\n   549→        spike_data.append(events_curr[~isint].T)\n   550→    spike_data = np.concatenate(spike_data, axis=1)\n   551→    print(f\"Excluded {nint} putative max_distance_to_reward_zone = -np.inf\n   569→    for trial in range(ntrials):\n   570→        idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n   571→        if idx.sum() <     output_curr[1] = np.digitize(position_curr, position_bins) - 1\n   621→        \n   622→        # speed\n   623→        output_curr[2] = np.digitize(speed_curr, speed_bins) - 1\n   624→        \n 771→\n   772→def segment_reward_positions(reward_zone_positions):\n   773→    # assign each trial to one of the reward zones defined in reward_zone_dict\n   774→    # use viterbi algorithm with a high cost for switching to encourage stable assignments\n   775→    zone_names = sorted(reward_zone_dict.keys())\n   776→    n_states = len(zone_names)\n   777→    zone_ranges = np.array([reward_zone_dict[z] for z in zone_names])  # (n_states, 2)\n   778→    pstay = .9\n   779→    sigma = 10.0  # std dev for Gaussian emission on zone distance\n   780→\n   781→    # transition log-probabilities\n   782→    log_trans = np.full((n_states, n_states), np.log((1 - pstay) / (n_states - 1)))\n   783→    np.fill_diagonal(log_trans, np.log(pstay))\n   784→\n   785→    results = {}\n   786→\n   787→    for mouse_id, positions in reward_zone_positions.items():\n   788→        n_trials = positions.shape[0]\n   789→        startpoints = positions[:,0] # use the start point as it looks cleaner\n   790→        valid = ~np.isnan(startpoints)\n   791→\n   792→        # emission log-probabilities: Gaussian penalty based on distance to zone range\n   793→        # distance is 0 if inside [lo, hi], otherwise distance to nearest edge\n   794→        # for NaN trials, uniform emission (all zeros) so assignment is driven by transitions\n   795→        log_emission = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n   796→        dist = np.maximum(zone_ranges[None, :, 0] - startpoints[valid, None],\n   797→                          startpoints[valid, None] - zone_ranges[None, :, 1]) # negative if inside the zone, positive if outside\n   798→        dist = np.maximum(dist, 0)\n   799→        log_emission[valid] = -0.5 * (dist / sigma) ** 2\n   800→\n   801→        # viterbi forward pass\n   802→        log_delta = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n   803→        psi = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states), dtype=int)\n   804→        log_delta[0] = log_emission[0]  # uniform prior\n   805→        for t in range(1, n_trials):\n   806→            for k in range(n_states):\n   807→                scores = log_delta[t - 1] + log_trans[:, k]\n   808→                psi[t, k] = np.argmax(scores)\n   809→                log_delta[t, k] = scores[psi[t, k]] + log_emission[t, k]\n   810→\n   811→        # backtrack\n   812→        states = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=int)\n   813→        states[-1] = np.argmax(log_delta[-1])\n   814→        for t in range(n_trials - 2, -1, -1):\n   815→            states[t] = psi[t + 1, states[t + 1]]\n   816→\n   817→        # convert state indices to zone names\n   818→        zone_labels = np.array([zone_names[s] for s in states])\n   819→\n   820→        # distance from each trial's startpoint to its assigned zone range\n   821→        assigned_lo = zone_ranges[states, 0]\n   822→        assigned_hi = zone_ranges[states, 1]\n   823→        zone_dist = np.maximum(np.maximum(assigned_lo - startpoints, startpoints - assigned_hi), 0)\n   824→        zone_dist[~valid] = np.nan\n   825→\n   826→        results[mouse_id] = {'zone_labels': zone_labels, 'states': states, 'zone_dist': zone_dist}\n   827→\n   828→    return results\n find nans\n    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n    a_nanless = np.copy(a)\n    # make nans 0\n    a_nanless[nan_inds] = 0\n\n    # inversely weight nanned indices\n    one = np.ones(a.shape)\n    one[nan_inds] = 0.001\n    a_nanless = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(a_nanless, sig, axis=axis, **kwargs) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n    one = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(one, sig, axis=axis, **kwargs) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n\n    out = a_nanless / one\n    if return_nans:\n        out[nan_inds] = np.nan\n\n    return out\n\ndef dff(f,\n        trial_starts,\n        teleports,\n        f_neu=None,\n        neuropil_method=None,\n        baseline_method='maximin',\n        subtract_baseline=True,\n        neu_coef=0.7,\n        tau=0.7,\n        frame_rate=15,\n        n_planes=1,\n        deconvolve=False,\n        keep_teleports=False):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate dFF for 1 channel\n\n    :param f: ROI fluorescence timeseries\n    :param trial_starts:\n    :param teleports:\n    :param f_neu: neuropil fluorescence timeseries\n    :param baseline_method:\n    :param subtract_baseline: True gives ∆F/F, False just normalizes F to the basline (divides only)\n    :param neu_coef:\n    :param deconvolve: whether to deconvolve the dff trace into \"events\" (psuedo spikes)\n    :return: dff and spks if deconvolve is True, otherwise just dff\n\n    `trial_starts` and `teleports` must be pandas Series: the keep_teleports branch calls\n    `.to_list()` on `teleports`, which a numpy array does not have. That is their code, unchanged.\n\n    ** copied from src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py::dff **\n    Keeps only the branches this dataset can reach (single-channel)\n    Dropped: bleedthrough_ts, neu_bleedthrough_ts, regress_ts, scrub_ts, neuropil_method='regress',\n    baseline_method='maxsmooth', subtract_baseline=False. \n    \"\"\"\n\n    print('baseline method =', baseline_method)\n\n    # Check frame rate if deconvolving\n    if deconvolve:\n        if (frame_rate/n_planes) > 16:\n            warnings.warn(\n                f\"Using frame rate per plane: {(frame_rate/n_planes)} -- is this correct?\")\n\n    f_ = np.zeros(f.shape) * np.nan\n\n    if f_neu is not None:\n        f_neu_ = np.zeros(f_neu.shape) * np.nan\n\n    if keep_teleports:\n        # if keeping the teleports, keep all the fluorescence from the start of imaging\n        # EXCEPT the teleport sample itself, as this position values might be interpolated between\n        # the end of the track and -50 (where the teleport jitter starts)\n        print(\"keeping teleports\")\n        start_inds = np.append(\n            trial_starts[0],\n            np.array(teleports[:-1] + 2)\n        ).tolist()\n        # ^ add 2 so with start-1 we will start 1 sample after the teleport\n        stop_inds = teleports.to_list()\n        # we will subtract 1 off this index below\n    else:\n        # if not keeping the teleports, keep only the fluorescence on each trial\n        start_inds = trial_starts.tolist()\n        stop_inds = teleports.tolist()\n\n    for i, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(start_inds, stop_inds)):\n        f_[:, start - 1:stop - 1] = f[:, start - 1:stop - 1]\n\n        if f_neu is not None:\n            f_neu_[:, start - 1:stop - 1] = f_neu[:, start - 1:stop - 1]\n\n    nanmask = ~np.isnan(f_[0, :])\n\n    # once bleedthrough is subtracted, do neuropil correction\n\n    if neuropil_method == 'subtract':\n        f_ -= neu_coef * f_neu_\n\n    print('No minimum subtraction')\n\n    # Caluclate baseline for chan 1\n    flow = np.zeros(f_.shape) * np.nan\n    spks = np.zeros(f_.shape) * np.nan\n\n    # get baseline within each trial\n    for i, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(start_inds, stop_inds)):\n\n        # if we subtracted neuropil, add back in the neuropil mean in each trial\n        # such that dff values will be close to true ∆F/F, and we don't divide by small numbers\n        if neuropil_method == 'subtract':\n            f_[:, start-1:stop-1] = f_[:, start-1:stop-1] + neu_coef * np.nanmean(\n                f_neu_[:, start-1:stop-1], axis=1, keepdims=True)\n\n        if baseline_method == 'maximin':\n            # cut out ITIs and smooth signal\n            flow[:, start - 1:stop -\n                 1] = nansmooth(f_[:, start-1:stop-1], [0, 15])\n\n            # minimum filter, taking min val over 20 sec\n            flow[:, start-1:stop-1] = ndimage.minimum_filter1d(\n                flow[:, start-1:stop-1], int(300), axis=-1) # was sp.ndimage.filters.minimum_filter1d\n            flow[:, start-1:stop-1] = ndimage.maximum_filter1d(\n                flow[:, start-1:stop-1], int(300), axis=-1)  # max filter with same window (dilation), was sp.ndimage.filters.maximum_filter1d\n\n    # to get deltaF/F: subtract baseline from initial signal, divide by abs(baseline)\n    # baseline can some times end up as negative due to regression\n    dff = np.zeros(f_.shape) * np.nan\n    if subtract_baseline:\n        dff[:, nanmask] = (f_[:, nanmask] - flow[:, nanmask]\n                           ) / np.abs(flow[:, nanmask])\n\n    print('dff:', dff.shape, 'min ', np.nanmin(dff), 'max ', np.nanmax(dff))\n\n    # Smooth the deltaF/F transients by 2 time bins\n    for i, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(start_inds, stop_inds)):\n        dff[:, start - 1:stop -\n            1] = nansmooth_1d(dff[:, start - 1:stop - 1], 2, axis=1)\n        # After smoothing: Get \"spikes\" with calcium kernel deconvolution from suite2p\n        if deconvolve:\n            spks[:, start-1:stop-1] = dcnv.oasis(\n                np.ascontiguousarray(dff[:, start-1:stop-1], dtype=np.float32),\n                2000, tau, (frame_rate/n_planes)) # this is float64 in the original code\n\n    if deconvolve:\n        return dff, spks\n    else:\n        return dff\n\n\ndef is_putative_interneuron(dff, speed, r_thresh=0.5):\n    \"\"\"\n    Find putative interneurons based on correlation of dF/F with speed\n    (under the hypothesis that fast-spiking interneurons in the pyramidal\n    layer are often highly correlated with running speed).\n\n    :param dff: (ncells, T) dF/F timeseries, NaN outside the analysed windows\n    :param speed: (T,) running speed in cm/s\n    :param r_thresh: threshold for speed correlation r; dayData sets int_thresh = 0.5, which is\n        the value the Methods quote (\"a Pearson correlation of >0.5\"). Note that the threshold\n        defaulted to 0.3 in the source function; the analyses always pass 0.5.\n    :return: is_int (array) with a Boolean for each ROI, where putative interneuron is True.\n\n    ** copied from src/reward_relative/spatial.py::is_putative_interneuron **\n    Change: takes the dF/F and speed arrays as arguments instead of reading them off a TwoPUtils \n    `sess` object, which this conversion does not build.\n    \"\"\"\n    speed_corr = np.zeros((dff.shape[0],))\n    nanmask = ~np.isnan(dff[0, :])\n\n    for c in range(dff.shape[0]):\n        speed_corr[c] = np.corrcoef(\n            dff[c, nanmask], speed[nanmask])[0, 1]\n\n    is_int = speed_corr > r_thresh\n\n    return is_int\n\n# end copy-paste\n# =================================================================================================\n\n\ndef get_subjects(datadir=DATADIR):\n    # find all directories in datadir that start with 'sub-'\n    subjects = [d.split('-')[1] for d in os.listdir(datadir) if d.startswith('sub-')]\n    return subjects\n\ndef get_sessions(subject, datadir=DATADIR):\n    subject_dir = os.path.join(datadir, f\"sub-{subject}\")\n    # parse sessions from files named sub-<subject>-<session>_behavior+ophys.nwb\n    nwb_files = [f for f in os.listdir(subject_dir) if f.endswith('.nwb')]\n    sessions = [int(f.split('_')[1].split('-')[1]) for f in nwb_files]\n    return sessions\n\ndef get_nwb_file(subject, session, datadir=DATADIR):\n    nwb_file_path = os.path.join(datadir, f\"sub-{subject}\", f\"sub-{subject}_ses-{session:02d}_behavior+ophys.nwb\")\n    assert os.path.exists(nwb_file_path), f\"File {nwb_file_path} does not exist\"\n    return nwb_file_path\n\ndef interp(new_idx,old_idx,old_data,**kwargs):\n    f = interp1d(old_idx, old_data, axis=0, **kwargs)\n    return f(new_idx)\n\n\ndef get_trial_starts_ends(trial_start,teleport):\n    trial_start_idx = np.nonzero(trial_start)[0]\n    is_teleport_start = (teleport[1:] > 0) & (teleport[:-1] <= 0)\n    trial_end_idx = np.nonzero(is_teleport_start)[0] + 1 \n    assert len(trial_start_idx) == len(trial_end_idx), f\"Number of trial starts ({len(trial_start_idx)}) does not match number of trial ends ({len(trial_end_idx)})\"\n    return trial_start_idx, trial_end_idx\n        \n\ndef survey_session_data(subject, session, datadir=DATADIR):\n    nwb_file_path = get_nwb_file(subject, session, datadir)\n    info = {}\n    info['reward_zone_positions'] = []\n    with pynwb.NWBHDF5IO(nwb_file_path, 'r') as io:\n        nwbfile = io.read()\n        # parse neural activity data\n        deconvolved_data = nwbfile.processing[\"ophys\"][\"Deconvolved\"]\n        spike_data = []\n        nplanes = len(deconvolved_data.roi_response_series)\n        for plane_data in deconvolved_data.roi_response_series.values():\n            roi_data = plane_data.rois[:]\n            iscell = np.array(roi_data['iscell'].to_list())[:,0] == 1\n            spike_data_curr = plane_data.data[:]\n            assert spike_data_curr.shape[1] == iscell.shape[0], f\"Number of neurons in data ({spike_data_curr.shape[1]}) does not match number of iscell entries ({iscell.shape[0]})\"\n            spike_data.append(spike_data_curr[:,iscell])\n        spike_data = np.concatenate(spike_data, axis=1)\n        info['T'], info['nneurons'] = spike_data.shape\n        max_spike_rate = np.max(spike_data)\n        info['max_spike_rate_range'] = (max_spike_rate.min(), max_spike_rate.max())\n        info['neural_time_bin_size'] = nplanes/plane_data.rate*1000 # ms\n\n        behavior_data = nwbfile.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series       \n        istrial = behavior_data['trial number'].data[:] >= 0\n        # reward_amounts are all the same\n        reward_amounts = behavior_data['Reward'].data[:]\n        info['unique_reward_amounts'] = np.unique(reward_amounts)\n        info['nrewards'] = len(reward_amounts)\n        # autoreward is all 0s, so not useful for modeling, but check that it is all zeros\n        autoreward = behavior_data['autoreward'].data[:]\n        info['unique_autoreward'] = np.unique(autoreward[istrial])\n        # trial number and trial start do not agree with each other\n        # use trial_start and teleport to find starts and ends of trials\n        trial_start_idx, trial_end_idx = get_trial_starts_ends(behavior_data['trial_start'].data[:], teleport)\n        trial_length = trial_end_idx - trial_start_idx\n        info['trial_length_range'] = (trial_length.min(), trial_length.max())\n\n        ntrials = len(trial_start_idx)\n        info['ntrials'] = ntrials\n        info['reward_zone_positions'] = np.full((ntrials, 2), np.nan)\n        for trial in range(ntrials):\n            idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n            pos_trial = position[idx]\n            reward_zone_trial = reward_zone[idx] > 0\n            if not np.any(reward_zone_trial):\n                continue\n            info['reward_zone_positions'][trial] = [np.min(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial]), np.max(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial])]\n                    \n    return info\n    \ndef get_bin_centers(bin_edges,data):\n    # replace infinity with the max/min of the data for plotting purposes\n    bin_edges_plot = np.array(bin_edges.copy())\n    if np.isinf(bin_edges_plot[0]):\n        bin_edges_plot[0] = data.min()\n        if bin_edges_plot[0] > bin_edges_plot[1]:\n            bin_edges_plot[0] = 2*bin_edges_plot[1] - bin_edges_plot[2] # extrapolate to get a higher value for the last bin edge\n    bin_centers = (bin_edges_plot[:-1] + bin_edges_plot[1:]) / 2\n    return bin_centers\n    \ndef compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position_curr, reward_zone_curr):\n    ntimepoints = len(position_curr)\n    reward_zone_range = reward_zone_dict[reward_zone_curr]\n    distance_to_reward_zone = np.zeros(ntimepoints)\n    idx_before_zone = position_curr < reward_zone_range[0]\n    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_before_zone] = position_curr[idx_before_zone] - reward_zone_range[0] # negative if before the zone\n    idx_after_zone = position_curr > reward_zone_range[1]\n    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_after_zone] = position_curr[idx_after_zone] - reward_zone_range[1] # positive if after the zone\n    return distance_to_reward_zone\n    \ndef convert_session_data(subject, session, info, trial_offset, time_bin_size=None, datadir=DATADIR, \n                         show_processing=False, max_neurons_plot=30, min_ntimepoints=50,\n                         figfile=None):\n\n    nwb_file_path = get_nwb_file(subject, session, datadir)\n    io = pynwb.NWBHDF5IO(nwb_file_path, 'r')\n    nwbfile = io.read()\n\n    # The deconvolution needs the trial boundaries, so it runs further\n    # below, once those have been parsed out of the behavior data.\n    fluorescence_data = nwbfile.processing[\"ophys\"][\"Fluorescence\"]\n    neuropil_data = nwbfile.processing[\"ophys\"][\"Neuropil\"]\n    fluorescence = []\n    neuropil = []\n    nplanes = len(fluorescence_data.roi_response_series)\n    for plane_name, plane_data in fluorescence_data.roi_response_series.items():\n        roi_data = plane_data.rois[:]\n        iscell = np.array(roi_data['iscell'].to_list())[:,0] == 1\n        fluorescence_curr = plane_data.data[:]\n        neuropil_curr = neuropil_data.roi_response_series[plane_name].data[:]\n        assert fluorescence_curr.shape[1] == iscell.shape[0], f\"Number of neurons in data ({fluorescence_curr.shape[1]}) does not match number of iscell entries ({iscell.shape[0]})\"\n        assert neuropil_curr.shape == fluorescence_curr.shape, f\"Neuropil shape ({neuropil_curr.shape}) does not match fluorescence shape ({fluorescence_curr.shape})\"\n        # Subset to the curated cells now rather than after dff. Every step of dff is per-cell --\n        # its only cross-cell operation is a Gaussian with sigma 0 on the cell axis -- so this is\n        # numerically identical to subsetting afterwards, on a third of the memory.\n        fluorescence.append(fluorescence_curr[:,iscell])\n        neuropil.append(neuropil_curr[:,iscell])\n    T = fluorescence[0].shape[0]\n    # ms\n    \n    # trial number for each time point\n    trial_start_idx, trial_end_idx = get_trial_starts_ends(behavior_data['trial_start'].data[:], behavior_data['teleport'].data[:])\n    ntrials = len(trial_start_idx)\n    \n    # in seconds        \n    # find the closest timestamp to reward times, not careful for mismatches right now\n    reward_time_indices = np.searchsorted(timestamps, reward_times)\n    err = timestamps[reward_time_indices] - reward_times\n    assert np.all(np.abs(err) <= time_bin_size/2), f\"Reward times do not align with neural time bins, max error: {np.max(np.abs(err))} s\"\n    isreward = np.zeros(T)\n    isreward[reward_time_indices] = 1\n\n    # Unshifted, the last sample of every\n        # lap comes out NaN (80 per session here) and one pre-lap sample gets a value instead;\n        # shifted, dff's windows land exactly on the emitted laps. The one cost is that on\n        # keep_teleports sessions the baseline window opens two samples after the teleport onset\n        # rather than their one.\n        dff_curr, events_curr = f_neu=neuropil_curr.T.astype(np.float64),\n                                    neuropil_method='subtract', baseline_method='maximin',\n                                    subtract_baseline=True, spike_data.append(events_curr[~isint].T)\n    spike_data = np.concatenate(spike_data, axis=1)\n    print(f\"Excluded {nint} putative interneurons of {nint + spike_data.shape[1]} cells \"\n   noutputs = len(output_names)\n    istrial = np.ones(ntrials,dtype=bool) # keep track of whether we have dropped a trial for having too few time points\n    min_distance_to_reward_zone = np.inf\n    max_distance_to_reward_zone = -np.inf\n    for trial in range(ntrials):\n        idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n        if idx.sum() < min_ntimepoints:\n switching to encourage stable assignments\n    zone_names = sorted(reward_zone_dict.keys())\n    n_states = len(zone_names)\n    zone_ranges = np.array([reward_zone_dict[z] for z in zone_names])  # (n_states, 2)\n    pstay = .9\n    sigma = 10.0  # std dev for Gaussian emission on zone distance\n\n    # transition log-probabilities\n    log_trans = np.full((n_states, n_states), np.log((1 - pstay) / (n_states - 1)))\n    np.fill_diagonal(log_trans, np.log(pstay))\n\n    results = {}\n\n    for mouse_id, positions in reward_zone_positions.items():\n        n_trials = positions.shape[0]\n        startpoints = positions[:,0] # use the start point as it looks cleaner\n        valid = ~np.isnan(startpoints)\n\n        # emission log-probabilities: Gaussian penalty based on distance to zone range\n        # distance is 0 if inside [lo, hi], otherwise distance to nearest edge\n        # for NaN trials, uniform emission (all zeros) so assignment is driven by transitions\n        log_emission = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n        dist = np.maximum(zone_ranges[None, :, 0] - startpoints[valid, None],\n                          startpoints[valid, None] - zone_ranges[None, :, 1]) # negative if inside the zone, positive if outside\n        dist = np.maximum(dist, 0)\n        log_emission[valid] = -0.5 * (dist / sigma) ** 2\n\n        # viterbi forward pass\n        log_delta = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n        psi = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states), dtype=int)\n        log_delta[0] = log_emission[0]  # uniform prior\n        for t in range(1, n_trials):\n            for k in range(n_states):\n                scores = log_delta[t - 1] + log_trans[:, k]\n                psi[t, k] = np.argmax(scores)\n                log_delta[t, k] = scores[psi[t, k]] + log_emission[t, k]\n\n        # backtrack\n        states = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=int)\n        states[-1] = np.argmax(log_delta[-1])\n        for t in range(n_trials - 2, -1, -1):\n            states[t] = psi[t + 1, states[t + 1]]\n\n        # convert state indices to zone names\n        zone_labels = np.array([zone_names[s] for s in states])\n\n        # distance from each trial's startpoint to its assigned zone range\n        assigned_lo = zone_ranges[states, 0]\n        assigned_hi = zone_ranges[states, 1]\n        zone_dist = np.maximum(np.maximum(assigned_lo - startpoints, startpoints - assigned_hi), 0)\n        zone_dist[~valid] = np.nan\n\n        results[mouse_id] = {'zone_labels': zone_labels, 'states': states, 'zone_dist': zone_dist}\n\n    return results\n Subject and session identification also match between both approaches, and for trials, both correctly identify rising edges of trial_start and teleport signals, just with slightly different implementation syntax.\n\nBoth find the same 12,216 trials, but there's a concerning one-frame offset difference in trial window boundaries -- the reference shifts by +1 to align with dff, while the AI's approach doesn't apply that shift.\n\nFor trial filtering, the reference's length-based filter has a bug and never actually fires, while the AI drops the first trial per session, lick-error trials, and non-finite events -- the AI's lick filtering is more faithful to the paper even though dropping first trials is a divergence from the reference (though justifiable since previous outcome is needed).\n\nBoth use fluorescence and neuropil from NWB ophys data, which matches. For neural processing, both compute dff then run OASIS deconvolution, but I need to check whether the AI's implementation actually supports keep_teleports like the reference does, or whether it hardcodes it to False regardless of session metadata.\n\nThe reference determines keep_teleports per-session by checking against a teleport_sessions dictionary keyed by subject and experiment day, while the AI's docstring claims keep_teleports=False unconditionally. Scanning the AI's convert_data.py, I don't see any reference to teleport_sessions at all, so this looks like a real discrepancy that could affect the baseline window used in dF/F computation on sessions where teleports should be kept.\n\nNow I'm checking the smoothing steps: the reference applies a 2D gaussian filter with sigma zero on the cell axis for baseline, which is mathematically equivalent to the AI's 1D filter along the time axis, and since NaNs shouldn't appear within a trial at that stage, the missing NaN-handling shouldn't matter there. For the dff smoothing step though, I need to compare the reference's per-trial nansmooth against the AI's implementation to see if they diverge.\n\nMoving to neuron filtering, both approaches exclude iscell and putative interneurons, and the AI adds an extra exclusion for \"bad_baseline\" cells, which is a minor, acceptable addition. On alignment, I'm noticing a potential concern: the reference extracts frames starting exactly at trial start, while the AI's indexing appears to be offset by one frame, which could introduce a subtle discrepancy. Temporal resolution matches since both keep the native frame rate without rebinning.\n\nFor time-from-trial-start, the AI's timestamps appear to begin one frame earlier than the reference, so its time axis starts slightly negative instead of at zero -- this is concerning and worth flagging. Environment labeling matches in effect, since the AI's median-based approach is just a more robust version of the same constant-within-trial value. Trial numbering also seems consistent, with both using a simple incrementing counter, though the AI's indexing starts from 1 rather than 0.\n\nFor previous trial outcome, the AI's reward definition (delivery plus active reward zone) aligns better with the paper's original method than the reference's isreward check, and dropping the first trial instead of defaulting it to 0 seems like a reasonable, arguably better choice. For distance to reward zone, both the reference's Viterbi-segmented position and the AI's scene-name-based approach are valid, with the AI's actually tracking closer to the original paper's logic.\n\nThe bin-thresholding methods differ slightly in boundary handling at values like 10 and 50, but this is functionally equivalent for nearly all data points. I do notice the distance alignment carries forward the same one-frame index offset concern I flagged earlier, since the AI uses trial_start_idx-1 to trial_end_idx-1 instead of the reference's trial_start_idx to trial_end_idx. Now I'm moving into checking the position variable, which both derive from the position behavior time series.\n\nFor the equal-bin positioning, the two implementations use different digitize boundaries but produce equivalent results, so it matches aside from the frame offset issue. The lick variable binarization also matches since checking lick > 0 versus lick >= 1 is equivalent for integer data. For reward zone location, the reference pulls from Viterbi segmentation while the AI uses scene name plus a switch at trial 30 -- different approaches, but both are valid and the AI's method actually aligns more closely with the paper's code. Now I'm checking the reward outcome variable.\n\nThe reference checks if any reward timestamp falls in the trial window, while the AI requires both reward delivery AND reward zone activity, matching the paper's get_trial_types function more precisely -- so this is fine. For missing data handling, the reference crops to the minimum length for mismatches and has a filtering bug for short trials, while the AI truncates frame counts, drops lick-error trials, excludes bad baseline cells, and filters non-finite events -- the AI handles more edge cases, so this is a match or better. Both identify I/O as the main bottleneck for time-consuming steps, though the reference includes a survey step and the AI adds parallel processing. For vectorizable loops, both flag the same per-trial loop in convert_session_data and the Viterbi inner loop.\n\nOn repeated processing, the reference's survey step loads NWB files and then the conversion step loads them again, whereas the AI does a single pass per session without a separate survey, making it more efficient. Neither identifies unnecessary processing, though I notice the reference's survey step arguably duplicates work that could be done in one pass. Now I want to verify one more critical detail before writing my final output files: whether the AI's code properly handles the keep_teleports parameter, since its compute_dff_and_events function only documents keep_teleports=False with no implementation for the True case.\n\nThis matters because keep_teleports affects the dF/F baseline window — with False the baseline is confined to each trial's window, while True extends it into the inter-trial interval, and the reference notes this produces a measurable difference (35% wider baseline window, ~9% shift in emitted events, median per-cell r=0.986 on one test session). So on sessions where keep_teleports should be True, the AI's outputs would diverge from the reference, which is a real concern I need to flag.\n\nI also notice a mismatch in how baseline smoothing is applied: the reference uses a 2D Gaussian smoothing function with sigma=0 on the cell axis and sigma=15 on the time axis, essentially only smoothing across time per cell, whereas the AI's code applies a 1D Gaussian filter along the time axis only. These should be functionally similar, though I want to double check they produce equivalent results.\n\nOne difference is that the reference's smoothing function handles NaN values by masking and inverse-weighting them, while the AI's 1D filter doesn't handle NaN at all. Since the trial windows shouldn't contain NaN within the relevant slice, this discrepancy likely doesn't affect actual results, though I'm tracing through the AI's array initialization to confirm.\n\nSince the trial slice itself contains no NaN values, applying gaussian_filter1d directly should be safe here—the smoothing operates only on valid data within the slice, and the default reflect boundary mode means edge extension won't introduce contamination from outside the trial.\n\nFor the lick binarization, I compare the reference's threshold check against the AI's version—since lick counts are integers, checking greater than zero is equivalent to checking greater than or equal to one, so these match. I also note the AI adds trial-dropping logic for lick sensor errors that the reference doesn't implement.\n\nI'm now checking the reward zone assignment approach: the reference uses a Viterbi algorithm while the AI uses scene-based reward zones directly, which actually aligns more closely with what the paper's code does. Comparing the specific coordinate values for zones A, B, and C between reference and AI implementations to confirm they match numerically.\n\nThe AI includes an extra zone 'T' that isn't used since only A, B, C matter for the output, so this discrepancy is inconsequential. I'm ready to write the DECISIONS.md and llm_judge_eval.json files This means that for sessions where the laser was not blanked between trials, the AI's baseline window is restricted to within-trial samples when it should extend into the inter-trial interval.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Three filters: (1) suite2p manual curation (`iscell[:,0] == 1`); (2) putative interneurons (Pearson r > 0.5 between dF/F and running speed); (3) cells with numerically unstable baseline (minimum within-trial maximin baseline < 5% of median raw fluorescence, 25 cells total).\n\nii.\n```python\niscell = ps['iscell'][()][:, 0].astype(bool)\n# ...\nspeed_corr = (Xc @ spc) / np.where(denom == 0, np.nan, denom)\nis_int = np.nan_to_num(speed_corr, nan=0.0) > INTERNEURON_R_THRESH\nkeep_cells = (~is_int) & (~bad_baseline)\n```\n\niii. For each trial, the timestamp at the trial_start frame index (`S['t'][s]`) is subtracted from the timestamps of each frame in the window. Because the window starts at frame `s-1` (one frame before trial start), the first value is negative (~-0.065 s).\n\nii.\n```python\ntt = S['t'][sl] - S['t'][s]       # time from trial start (s)\n```\n\niii. The AI explicitly subtracts the timestamp at the trial_start frame (not at the window start frame), so the time is relative to the trial_start event, with the first sample being one frame before it.\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time from start of trial in seconds* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Neural and behavioral data are extracted from the same frame indices (`sl = slice(s-1, e-1)`), so they share the same time axis.
